MASTER OF MAGIC v1.31 (WIZARDS.EXE) - ORIGINAL-GAME BUGS AND QUIRKS FOUND WHILE DECOMPILING
Consolidated 2026-10-16 (diplomacy 46-67 step 38; items/units 68-93 step 39; info/lists 94-101 step 40). Every item comes from reading the code; only the GAME ones were also seen in game.
Status:  CHECKED = I verified the instructions myself in the assembly (tools/dis16.py);
         SRC     = re-checked by me in the matched source (src/, which compiles byte-identical to the shipped
                   EXE): the entry's 'Re-checked (N221)' line names the code (P4 B1); #413+ come from our
                   own notes on that source;
         READ    = found by a reader agent from decompile + assembly, not re-checked by me (none left after
                   N221: all became SRC);
         READ+asm= found by a reader agent who verified it in the assembly (dis16) - not re-checked by me;
         WIKI    = from the community MoM wiki (masterofmagic.fandom.com; its numbers come from the 1.50 patch
                   authors reading the code), not yet checked by us;  OPEN = we and the wiki disagree, to settle;
         GAME    = confirmed in game (since P4 B4: an 'In game' line names the run, tools/harness/regress.py).
         SAME    = the same bug as the entry it names (merged there in N221); the number stays because src
                   cites it.
Details / context: ORIGINAL_NOTES.txt (section named in brackets) and work/reports/*.tsv.

=== EVENTS / END OF TURN ===
1. CHECKED  Depletion and New Minerals random events can NEVER happen. The site search loops "while result == 0" but the
            result starts at -1 (cbdf:0db1 / 0f2e, Event_Find_Depletion_Site / Event_Find_New_Mineral_Site). [END TURN -
            SECOND WAVE]  Test: hundreds of turns should never show either event.
            Static check (2026-10-24, tools/harness/regress.py 1): wizards/TERRCHG.C:417 holds the code;
            Event_Find_Depletion_Site = 1.31 bytes; Event_Find_New_Mineral_Site = 1.31 bytes
            [work/harness/game/WIZARDS.EXE]
2. GAME     City_Remove fall-through (c3f5:157a): after an outpost is removed the loop does not "continue", so the next
            city in that slot can be turned into population 1 / size 1 and skips its growth that turn.
            Re-checked (N221): City_Remove shifts the records; the shifted city is tested by the population_tens >= 10
            branch and skips its own turn.
            In game (2026-10-24, tools/harness/regress.py 2): turn 107: Sunnydale removed; Zho now index 9, population
            1, size 1
3. GAME     AI wizards use the HUMAN's fame for merchant / mercenary / hero offers (c3f5:1e19). [MAIN LOOP + NEXT TURN]
            Re-checked (N221): fame is assigned only in the human branch; the AI iterations reuse it.
            In game (2026-10-24, tools/harness/regress.py 3): mercenary rolls (player, chance) [(0, 10), (1, 20), (2,
            20), (4, 20)]; the chance from each AI's own fame 0 would be {1: 11, 2: 11, 3: 11, 4: 11} (Famous {1: 0,
            2: 0, 3: 0, 4: 0})

            test reads p_cities[14] (DI = the option count), population 7

9. GAME     AI-vs-AI treaty roll (AI_Diplo_Turn_AI_Vs_AI 7269:04c5, phase-2 re-read): with S = bias + relation +
            personality + Random(100) + (Random(5)+3) x difficulty (+10 pact, +20 alliance) and T = treaty interest, S +
            T >= 151 gives an ALLIANCE from no treaty (a pact is skipped) and even from WAR (status 3 -> 2 with no peace
            made); an existing alliance is turned back into a pact; a pact becomes an alliance. Below 151 they trade
            spells when S > trade interest + 80 (so a higher trade interest makes a trade LESS likely), and the trade
            passes crossed arguments (no spell moves but the Mastery cost still drops, aba4:05e2). COMMUNITY-KNOWN:
            W078DIPL lists the same four, W540FIX (AI can't trade).
            Re-checked (N221): DIPLOMSG.C AI_Diplo_Turn_AI_Vs_AI: score + treaty interest > 150 and not allied ->
            alliance (from none, pact or war), allied -> pact; else trade_interest + 80 < score trades (#458 crossed
            gifts); Wizard_Learn_Spell (TURNUPD.C) cuts the Mastery cost even for a spell already known.
            In game (2026-10-23, tools/harness/regress.py 9): turn 106: wizards 4 and 2 are made allies while their
            status is 3 (3 = war)
10. GAME    Subversion also lowers the AI caster's own relation with the target (af2c:2741; the wiki says it should not).
            Re-checked (N221): Subversion_Apply skips only the human and the victim, so an AI caster lowers its own

            prefers sites the human has explored. Matters for AI-vs-AI.

            human's) has it explored; AIUNITS.C 1905-1910 ignores *unexplored. The entry's 'only founds cities on
            tiles the human has explored' overstated it (corrected after the Codex review: unexplored squares add

206. READ+asm Random(n) (1d4d:00d8) draws only 9 bits per call (9 LFSR steps into a 0..511 value) and returns value % n
            + 1: results above 512 are impossible, and every roll has modulo bias - Random(100) gives 1..12 with 6/512
            and 13..100 with 5/512, so 'Random(100) <= 10' is 11.7% (not 10%) and '<= 50' 51.2%. The developers knew the
            limit (Random_Weighted_Pick and Event_Pick_Wizard halve weights until the sum is below 512); no call site
            passes a constant above 512 (Random(512) is the largest). Zero seed is replaced by 0x30be; n = 0 -> fatal
            'RND no 0's'.
            Static check (2026-10-24, tools/harness/regress.py 206): wizards/UTIL.C:99 holds the code; Random = 1.31
            bytes [work/harness/game/WIZARDS.EXE]
207. WITHDRAWN (step 78c-Z batch 19, byte-matched src/wizards/UTIL.C): NOT A BUG. Random_Get_Seed stores
            rng_state_hi (DS:7848) in the HIGH word of its long result and returns DX = high, AX = low; Random_Seed
            takes (low, high) = the long as pushed (AX lands at [bp+6]), so Random_Seed(Random_Get_Seed()) restores the
            state exactly. The original reading below misread which register holds which half. Original text:
     (old) Saving and restoring the random seed swaps its two halves: Random_Get_Seed (1d4d:0089) returns DX = low
            word, AX = high word, while Random_Seed (1d4d:0078) takes (low, high) and callers pass (AX, DX). So
            Outpost_Draw_Scene (cd8b:42f8: push [bp-8]=DX, push [bp-0xa]=AX), and (by the decompile; the other 6 save /
            restore sites not checked) Next_Turn's turn_rng_seed (stored end of turn, reseeded at the start of the next)
            put the generator back in a different (half-swapped) state. Still deterministic; it only defeats the intent
            that drawing does not disturb the game's random sequence.

339. LATENT  The clipped picture decoders lose their data segment on frames larger than 63 KB. Pic_Blit_Clipped_Planar
            (23a4:0087), Pic_Blit_Clipped_Planar_Remap (23a4:02e0) and Pic_Draw_Clipped_EMS (copy at linear 14344) re-base
            the read pointer at the start of each column once SI reaches 0xFC00. The three instructions are in the wrong
            order: 'mov ds, cx / add cx, 0xfc0 / mov cx, ds' (bytes 8E D9 81 C1 C0 0F 8C D9). The intent was 'mov cx, ds
            / add cx, 0xfc0 / mov ds, cx'. DS becomes CX instead, normally 0 after the previous column's loop, so the
            remaining columns are decoded from the interrupt vector table. That draws garbage, and its skip counts can
            push writes into the other VRAM pages. Reach: only frames of 0xFC00+ bytes drawn through a CLIPPED draw
            (Draw_Pic_Clipped and friends). A read-only scan of all 101 LBX files (15,792 frames) finds 7 such frames: 5
            in INTRO.LBX (MAGIC.EXE), SPELLSCR 13 (64,909 bytes - the item forge background, drawn with the UNCLIPPED
            Draw_Pic_Frame, whose decoder never re-bases and is fine below 64 KB) and RELOAD 25 (no WIZARDS loader
            found). So no shipped picture is known to trigger it in WIZARDS. MAGIC.EXE has the same three byte
            sequences; no community patch touches them. [CLIPPED DECODER SEGMENT WRAP]
            CORRECTION (78c-Z, byte-matching): the EMS copy is in Pic_Draw_Clipped_EMS_Remap (23f7:03da), not in
            Pic_Draw_Clipped_EMS (that one moves the EMS window at C000h instead). Sources: PICCLIP.ASM, PICEMS.ASM.
            Static check (2026-10-23, tools/harness/regress.py 339): wizards/PICCLIP.ASM:9 holds the code;
            Pic_Blit_Clipped_Planar = 1.31 bytes; Pic_Blit_Clipped_Planar_Remap = 1.31 bytes;
            Pic_Draw_Clipped_EMS_Remap = 1.31 bytes [work/harness/game/WIZARDS.EXE]

            and switches on a 3 x 3 neighbour index whose cases are laid out for (dy + 1) * 3 + (dx + 1) (0..8: diagonals
            need a non-ocean square beside them, straight neighbours always, 4 = the centre refused) - but the code
            computes dy * 3 + dx (asm 4358:04cd imul 3 / add / inc, then dec for the table at 04e6). Index < 0 (the
            four directions with dy = -1, or dy = 0 and dx = -1) fails the unsigned 'index > 8' test and is skipped;
            (1, 0) -> case 1 and (0, 1) -> case 3 always convert; (-1, 1) -> case 2, the north-east diagonal test, so it
            looks at the unrelated squares (x - 1, y + 2) and (x - 2, y + 1); (1, 1) -> case 4, the centre: refused. So
            such islands only ever grow to the east, south and south-west. [ISLAND SPECIALS]

            unsigned (jnc / jbe at 0dde / 0dfe). min never moves, so the "below the clip rect" reject (signed jg,
            0eb5) never fires; max moves only for a y above 10000 unsigned, i.e. a NEGATIVE y, and then the signed
            "max < clip_y1" test (jge, 0ebe) rejects the frame. (The min / max scan also runs inside the corner
            bubble sort, so a corner already moved past the scan index is not seen.) Only caller: Lose_Redraw's
            spinning portrait; its path table lose_anim_path (DS:0x511c) puts the top-left at y = -4, -10, -8, -5
            for steps 7-10, so frames around there are not drawn whenever a rotated corner stays above row 0
            (which steps depends on the portrait size - not computed). Not checked in game. Step 78c c61. [Gfx]
            Step 78c-Z4: confirmed by byte-matching src/wizards/PIC.C (the source sets max_y = 10000, min_y = 0 and
            scans inside the outer loop of the corner bubble sort; also MAGIC 1ee7).
            Re-checked (N221): our source note at src/wizards/PIC.C:737 states it.
            Static check (2026-10-24, tools/harness/regress.py 378): wizards/PIC.C:893 holds the code;
            Pic_Draw_Warped_Core = 1.31 bytes [work/harness/game/WIZARDS.EXE]

            unless a Spell Blast would also be valid - the AI's versions almost never work. COMMUNITY-KNOWN (W239FIX).
            In game (2026-10-23, tools/harness/regress.py 102): AI_Pick_Wizard_Target(spell 171, caster 1) = 0; wizard
            1 casting now 0
103. GAME    Growth only stops when population EQUALS the maximum (c751:139f cmp / jne): a city above its maximum (e.g.
            after Raise Volcano / Change Terrain shrinks its food area) still gets (max-pop+1)/2 + building and Housing
            bonuses and can keep growing. COMMUNITY-KNOWN (wiki "Population Growth"; the Insecticide patch fix). The
            same test also hides starvation AT the maximum: when population == max (c751:139f cmp / jne -> jmp 137f) the
            function returns 0 before the food < population branch, so a starving city at its maximum does not shrink.
            COMMUNITY-KNOWN (W220FIX: 'negative growth does not get applied if max population is reached').
            In game (2026-10-23, tools/harness/regress.py 103): turn 107: Cornwell (maximum 5) population 8 -> 8, tens
            0 -> 4, food 17

byte patch mapped onto our functions by tools/patch_map.py):
- A: 58 of our 101 items are fixed by a community patch too (several at the very same instruction, e.g. 46 = W082DIPL,
  9 = W540FIX, 1 = W272FIX); 39 have no community fix; 4 more are rewritten without naming the bug.
- B: about 310 community bug fixes are NOT in this list yet - only 18 in still-unnamed functions, i.e. most are in
  areas we already read: our readers catch only part of the bugs. They are UNVERIFIED by us; see section B (grouped
  by area). To promote one here: read the code, then add it with CHECKED / READ+asm and "COMMUNITY-KNOWN (<patch>)".
- C: 19 disagreements between our notes and the patch texts (both claims listed there).

            In game (2026-10-23, tools/harness/regress.py 408): at Death_Wish_Cast's RETF (byte 0xCB at 3ED0:02AC,
            0xCB = RETF): AX = 0 (0x0000), the value Overland_Cast_Spell takes as ok; wizard 1's Nature's Wrath 2 at
            that moment; Natures_Wrath_Strike calls (caster) []

=== FOUND BY THE REGRESSION SUITE (P4 B4) ===
489. GAME    Nightshade dispels its own count. City_Nightshade_Dispel (b425:1d2f), src/wizards/OVERSPEL.C, rolls every
            city enchantment slot 0..25 whose value is not the owner + 1 - slot 25 included, which is the Nightshade
            count itself; CitySlot_To_Spell(25) finds no spell, so the count is rolled as spell 0 at chance count x
            25000 / (resistance + count x 100) and usually zeroed. Our names database called this harmless because
            Next_Turn recounts the slot (Cities_Count_Nightshade), but the recount covers only cities with a
            qualifying building: a city that no longer qualifies loses its stale count here (see #482) - unless the
            count equals its owner + 1, which is never rolled and stays. Found by the regression suite (N224).
            In game (2026-10-22, tools/harness/regress.py 489): turn 107: Cremona Nightshade count 0 (set to 3)
490. SRC     The plain desert square gives no production bonus. Terrain_Production_Pct (f319:01da), src/wizards/TERRAIN.C,
            gives +3% to TILE_DESERT_2 / _3 / _4 and to the desert-edge range, but leaves TILE_DESERT (165, the single
            plain desert picture) out of its list, so that square adds 0%. The Surveyor's terrain text prints
            "+3% production" for every desert (SURVEYOR.C Surveyor_Terrain_Text, TERR_CAT_DESERT), so the display and
            the rules disagree for that one picture. (The manual's Table D says +10% for desert; its production
            figures differ from the game for every terrain - the game's own 3 / 5 % agree with the Surveyor.) Found
            10/23 while comparing the Surveyor texts with the rules for #272.
491. GAME    Inside a diplomacy audience the landmass map holds music. Diplo_Screen_Load (6ea7:14f3),
            src/wizards/DIPLOSCR.C, writes the world block to COMBAT.TMP (Combat_Tmp_Save) and then loads the
            partner's Good / Bad music (MUSIC.LBX 41 / 42 + 2 x portrait) into that block, over the map layers;
            Combat_Tmp_Restore puts them back when the audience ends. Diplo_Army_Near_Fortress (6ea7:1922) reads
            p_landmass_ids in between, so the army terms of a threat (Diplo_Threaten_Menu) and of the human's
            proposals (Diplo_Proposal_Response, DIPLOACT.C) count the units whose square holds the same MUSIC byte
            as the partner's fortress square, not the units on its continent: #50 / #51 describe the formula, but
            the armies fed to it inside an audience are arbitrary (fixed per partner portrait). AI_War_Check
            (DIPLOTRN.C) runs outside an audience and reads the real map. Found by the regression suite (N234):
            wizard 1 (portrait 1) - the 4800 bytes are the end of MUSIC entry 43 and the start of entry 44 (4714 of
            them differ from the map; the fortress square (49,14) Myrror reads 152 instead of landmass 14);
            COMBAT.TMP holds the real map and leaving restores it exactly.
            In game (2026-10-23, tools/harness/regress.py 491): fortress square (49,14) Myrror: 14 on the map, 152 in
            the audience, 14 after leaving; 4714 of 4800 bytes differ in the audience; Myrror half = MUSIC entry 44
            from byte 1040: True; restored exactly: True
